```
1→"""Scan reporting: score breakdown, delta helpers, and detector progress."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from collections.abc import Callable
     6→from typing import Protocol
     7→
     8→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
     9→# Protocol stubs for dependency-injected modules
    10→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    11→
    12→
    13→class _StateMod(Protocol):
    14→    def path_scoped_findings(self, findings: dict, scan_path: object) -> dict: ...
    15→
    16→
    17→class _NarrativeMod(Protocol):
    18→    STRUCTURAL_MERGE: frozenset[str]
    19→
    20→
    21→class _RegistryMod(Protocol):
    22→    DETECTORS: dict
    23→
    24→    def display_order(self) -> list[str]: ...
    25→
    26→
    27→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    28→# Breakdown helpers (from scan_reporting_breakdown)
    29→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    30→
    31→
    32→def dimension_bar(score: float, *, colorize_fn, bar_len: int = 15) -> str:
    33→    """Render a score bar consistent with scan detector bars."""
    34→    filled = max(0, min(bar_len, round(score / 100 * bar_len)))
    35→    if score >= 98:
    36→        return colorize_fn("█" * bar_len, "green")
    37→    if score >= 93:
    38→        return colorize_fn("█" * filled, "green") + colorize_fn(
    39→            "░" * (bar_len - filled), "dim"
    40→        )
    41→    return colorize_fn("█" * filled, "yellow") + colorize_fn(
    42→        "░" * (bar_len - filled), "dim"
    43→    )
    44→
    45→
    46→def show_score_model_breakdown(
    47→    state: dict,
    48→    *,
    49→    scoring_mod,
    50→    colorize_fn,
    51→    dim_scores: dict | None = None,
    52→) -> None:
    53→    """Show score recipe and weighted drags."""
    54→    if dim_scores is None:
    55→        dim_scores = state.get("dimension_scores", {})
    56→    if not dim_scores:
    57→        return
    58→
    59→    breakdown = scoring_mod.compute_health_breakdown(dim_scores)
    60→    mech_frac = float(breakdown.get("mechanical_fraction", 1.0) or 0.0)
    61→    subj_frac = float(breakdown.get("subjective_fraction", 0.0) or 0.0)
    62→    mech_avg = float(breakdown.get("mechanical_avg", 100.0) or 100.0)
    63→    subj_avg_raw = breakdown.get("subjective_avg")
    64→    subj_avg = float(subj_avg_raw) if isinstance(subj_avg_raw, int | float) else None
    65→    entries = [
    66→        entry for entry in breakdown.get("entries", []) if isinstance(entry, dict)
    67→    ]
    68→    if not entries:
    69→        return
    70→
    71→    print(colorize_fn("  Score recipe:", "dim"))
    72→    if subj_avg is None or subj_frac <= 0.0:
    73→        print(colorize_fn("    overall = 100% mechanical", "dim"))
    74→        print(colorize_fn(f"    Mechanical pool average: {mech_avg:.1f}%", "dim"))
    75→    elif mech_frac <= 0.0:
    76→        print(colorize_fn("    overall = 100% subjective", "dim"))
    77→        print(colorize_fn(f"    Subjective pool average: {subj_avg:.1f}%", "dim"))
    78→    else:
    79→        print(
    80→            colorize_fn(
    81→                f"    overall = {mech_frac * 100:.0f}% mechanical + {subj_frac * 100:.0f}% subjective",
    82→                "dim",
    83→            )
    84→        )
    85→        print(
    86→            colorize_fn(
    87→                f"    Pool averages: mechanical {mech_avg:.1f}% · subjective {subj_avg:.1f}%",
    88→                "dim",
    89→            )
    90→        )
    91→
    92→    drags = sorted(
    93→        [
    94→            entry
    95→            for entry in entries
    96→            if float(entry.get("overall_drag", 0.0) or 0.0) > 0.01
    97→        ],
    98→        key=lambda entry: -float(entry.get("overall_drag", 0.0) or 0.0),
    99→    )
   100→    if drags:
   101→        print(colorize_fn("    Biggest weighted drags:", "dim"))
   102→        for entry in drags[:5]:
   103→            name = str(entry.get("name", "unknown"))
   104→            score = float(entry.get("score", 0.0) or 0.0)
   105→            drag = float(entry.get("overall_drag", 0.0) or 0.0)
   106→            pool = str(entry.get("pool", "unknown"))
   107→            pool_share = float(entry.get("pool_share", 0.0) or 0.0) * 100
   108→            print(
   109→                colorize_fn(
   110→                    f"      - {name}: -{drag:.2f} pts "
   111→                    f"(score {score:.1f}%, {pool_share:.1f}% of {pool} pool)",
   112→                    "dim",
   113→                )
   114→            )
   115→    print()
   116→
   117→
   118→def show_dimension_deltas(
   119→    prev: dict,
   120→    current: dict,
   121→    *,
   122→    scoring_mod,
   123→    colorize_fn,
   124→) -> None:
   125→    """Show which dimensions changed between scans (health and strict)."""
   126→    moved = []
   127→    for dim in scoring_mod.DIMENSIONS:
   128→        p = prev.get(dim.name, {})
   129→        n = current.get(dim.name, {})
   130→        if not p or not n:
   131→            continue
   132→        old_score = p.get("score", 100)
   133→        new_score = n.get("score", 100)
   134→        old_strict = p.get("strict", old_score)
   135→        new_strict = n.get("strict", new_score)
   136→        delta = new_score - old_score
   137→        strict_delta = new_strict - old_strict
   138→        if abs(delta) >= 0.1 or abs(strict_delta) >= 0.1:
   139→            moved.append(
   140→                (
   141→                    dim.name,
   142→                    old_score,
   143→                    new_score,
   144→                    delta,
   145→                    old_strict,
   146→                    new_strict,
   147→                    strict_delta,
   148→                )
   149→            )
   150→
   151→    if not moved:
   152→        return
   153→
   154→    print(colorize_fn("  Moved:", "dim"))
   155→    for name, old, new, delta, old_s, new_s, s_delta in sorted(
   156→        moved, key=lambda item: item[3]
   157→    ):
   158→        sign = "+" if delta > 0 else ""
   159→        color = "green" if delta > 0 else "red"
   160→        strict_str = ""
   161→        if abs(s_delta) >= 0.1:
   162→            s_sign = "+" if s_delta > 0 else ""
   163→            strict_str = colorize_fn(
   164→                f"  strict: {old_s:.1f}→{new_s:.1f}% ({s_sign}{s_delta:.1f}%)",
   165→                "dim",
   166→            )
   167→        print(
   168→            colorize_fn(
   169→                f"    {name:<22} {old:.1f}% → {new:.1f}%  ({sign}{delta:.1f}%)", color
   170→            )
   171→            + strict_str
   172→        )
   173→    print()
   174→
   175→
   176→def show_low_dimension_hints(
   177→    dim_scores: dict,
   178→    *,
   179→    scoring_mod,
   180→    colorize_fn,
   181→) -> None:
   182→    """Show actionable hints for dimensions below 50%."""
   183→    static_names = {dim.name for dim in scoring_mod.DIMENSIONS}
   184→
   185→    mechanical_hints = {
   186→        "File health": "run `desloppify show structural` — split large files",
   187→        "Code quality": "run `desloppify show smells` — fix code smells",
   188→        "Duplication": "run `desloppify show dupes` — deduplicate functions",
   189→        "Test health": "add tests for uncovered files: `desloppify show test_coverage`",
   190→        "Security": "run `desloppify show security` — fix security issues",
   191→    }
   192→
   193→    low = []
   194→    for name, data in dim_scores.items():
   195→        strict = data.get("strict", data.get("score", 100))
   196→        if strict < 50:
   197→            hint = (
   198→                mechanical_hints.get(name, "run `desloppify show` for details")
   199→                if name in static_names
   200→                else "run `desloppify review --run-batches --runner codex --parallel --scan-after-import` to assess"
   201→            )
   202→            low.append((name, strict, hint))
   203→
   204→    if not low:
   205→        return
   206→
   207→    low.sort(key=lambda item: item[1])
   208→    print(colorize_fn("  Needs attention:", "yellow"))
   209→    for name, score, hint in low:
   210→        print(colorize_fn(f"    {name} ({score:.0f}%) — {hint}", "yellow"))
   211→    print()
   212→
   213→
   214→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   215→# Detector progress (from scan_reporting_progress)
   216→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   217→
   218→
   219→def show_detector_progress(
   220→    state: dict,
   221→    *,
   222→    state_mod: _StateMod,
   223→    narrative_mod: _NarrativeMod,
   224→    registry_mod: _RegistryMod,
   225→    colorize_fn: Callable[[str, str], str],
   226→) -> None:
   227→    """Show per-detector progress bars."""
   228→    findings = state_mod.path_scoped_findings(state["findings"], state.get("scan_path"))
   229→    if not findings:
   230→        return
   231→
   232→    by_detector: dict[str, dict] = {}
   233→    for finding in findings.values():
   234→        detector = finding.get("detector", "unknown")
   235→        if detector in narrative_mod.STRUCTURAL_MERGE:
   236→            detector = "structural"
   237→        if detector not in by_detector:
   238→            by_detector[detector] = {"open": 0, "total": 0}
   239→        by_detector[detector]["total"] += 1
   240→        if finding["status"] == "open":
   241→            by_detector[detector]["open"] += 1
   242→
   243→    detector_order = [
   244→        registry_mod.DETECTORS[d].display
   245→        for d in registry_mod.display_order()
   246→        if d in registry_mod.DETECTORS
   247→    ]
   248→    order_map = {display: i for i, display in enumerate(detector_order)}
   249→    sorted_dets = sorted(by_detector.items(), key=lambda item: order_map.get(item[0], 99))
   250→
   251→    print(colorize_fn("  Detector progress (open findings by detector):", "dim"))
   252→    print(colorize_fn("  " + "─" * 50, "dim"))
   253→    bar_len = 15
   254→    for detector, data in sorted_dets:
   255→        total = data["total"]
   256→        open_count = data["open"]
   257→        addressed = total - open_count
   258→        pct = round(addressed / total * 100) if total else 100
   259→
   260→        filled = round(pct / 100 * bar_len)
   261→        if pct == 100:
   262→            bar = colorize_fn("█" * bar_len, "green")
   263→        elif open_count <= 2:
   264→            bar = colorize_fn("█" * filled, "green") + colorize_fn(
   265→                "░" * (bar_len - filled), "dim"
   266→            )
   267→        else:
   268→            bar = colorize_fn("█" * filled, "yellow") + colorize_fn(
   269→                "░" * (bar_len - filled), "dim"
   270→            )
   271→
   272→        det_label = detector.replace("_", " ").ljust(18)
   273→        open_str = (
   274→            colorize_fn(f"{open_count:3d} open", "yellow")
   275→            if open_count > 0
   276→            else colorize_fn("  ✓", "green")
   277→        )
   278→        print(
   279→            f"  {det_label} {bar} {pct:3d}%  {open_str}  {colorize_fn(f'/ {total}', 'dim')}"
   280→        )
   281→
   282→    print()
   283→
   284→
   285→__all__ = [
   286→    "dimension_bar",
   287→    "show_dimension_deltas",
   288→    "show_detector_progress",
   289→    "show_low_dimension_hints",
   290→    "show_score_model_breakdown",
   291→]
   292→
```

```
   292→def compute_health_score(
   293→    dimension_scores: dict, *, score_key: str = "score"

   307→    subjective_assessments: dict | None = None,

   309→) -> ScoreBundle:
   310→    """Compute all score channels from one scoring engine pass."""
   311→    by_mode = compute_dimension_scores_by_mode(
   312→        findings,
   313→        potentials,
   314→        subjective_assessments=subjective_assessments,
   315→        allowed_subjective_dimensions=allowed_subjective_dimensions,

   317→
   318→    lenient_scores = by_mode["lenient"]
   319→    strict_scores = by_mode["strict"]

   321→
   322→    mechanical_lenient_scores = {

   324→        for name, data in lenient_scores.items()
   325→        if "subjective_assessment" not in data.get("detectors", {})
   326→    }
   327→
   328→    return ScoreBundle(
   329→        dimension_scores=lenient_scores,
   330→        strict_dimension_scores=strict_scores,
```

```
    28→def _detect(fixer: FixerConfig, path: Path) -> list[dict]:
    29→    print(colorize(f"\nDetecting {fixer.label}...", "dim"), file=sys.stderr)
    30→    entries = fixer.detect(path)
    31→    file_count = len(set(e["file"] for e in entries))
    32→    print(
    33→        colorize(
    34→            f"  Found {len(entries)} {fixer.label} across {file_count} files\n", "dim"
    35→        ),
    36→        file=sys.stderr,
    37→    )
    38→    return entries
    39→
    40→
    41→def _print_fix_summary(
    42→    fixer: FixerConfig,
    43→    results: list[dict],
    44→    total_items: int,
    45→    total_lines: int,
    46→    dry_run: bool,
    47→) -> None:
    48→    verb = fixer.dry_verb if dry_run else fixer.verb
    49→    lines_str = f" ({total_lines} lines)" if total_lines else ""
    50→    print(
    51→        colorize(
    52→            f"\n  {verb} {total_items} {fixer.label} across {len(results)} files{lines_str}\n",
    53→            "bold",
    54→        )
    55→    )
    56→    for r in results[:30]:
    57→        syms = ", ".join(r["removed"][:5])
    58→        if len(r["removed"]) > 5:
    59→            syms += f" (+{len(r['removed']) - 5})"
    60→        extra = f"  ({r['lines_removed']} lines)" if r.get("lines_removed") else ""
    61→        print(f"  {rel(r['file'])}{extra}  →  {syms}")
    62→    if len(results) > 30:
    63→        print(f"  ... and {len(results) - 30} more files")
    64→

    66→def _apply_and_report(

    76→) -> None:
    77→    state_file = state_path(args)
    78→    state = state_mod.load_state(state_file)
    79→    prev = state_mod.score_snapshot(state)
    80→    resolved_ids = _resolve_fixer_results(state, results, fixer.detector, fixer_name)

    89→            if overall_delta

    96→        )
```

```
    91→

    93→# Per-file weighted failures are capped at 1.0 to match the file-based denominator.
    94→FILE_BASED_DETECTORS = {

    97→    if policy.file_based

    99→
   100→
   101→def _build_dimensions() -> list[Dimension]:
   102→    """Derive dimensions from DETECTOR_SCORING_POLICIES.
   103→
   104→    Each unique (dimension, tier) pair becomes a Dimension, with its detectors
   105→    collected automatically. Order follows first-seen in the policies dict.
   106→    """
   107→    # Collect (dimension_name -> tier) preserving first-seen order,
   108→    # and group detectors by dimension.
   109→    dim_tiers: dict[str, int] = {}
   110→    grouped: dict[str, list[str]] = {}
   111→    for detector, policy in DETECTOR_SCORING_POLICIES.items():
   112→        if policy.dimension is None or policy.tier is None:
   113→            continue
   114→        if policy.dimension not in dim_tiers:
   115→            dim_tiers[policy.dimension] = policy.tier
   116→            grouped[policy.dimension] = []
   117→        grouped[policy.dimension].append(detector)
   118→    return [
   119→        Dimension(name=name, tier=tier, detectors=grouped[name])
   120→        for name, tier in dim_tiers.items()
   121→    ]
   122→
   123→
   124→DIMENSIONS = _build_dimensions()
   125→DIMENSIONS_BY_NAME = {d.name: d for d in DIMENSIONS}
   126→
   127→TIER_WEIGHTS = {
   128→    Tier.AUTO_FIX: 1,
   129→    Tier.QUICK_FIX: 2,
   130→    Tier.JUDGMENT: 3,
   131→    Tier.MAJOR_REFACTOR: 4,
   132→}
   133→CONFIDENCE_WEIGHTS = {Confidence.HIGH: 1.0, Confidence.MEDIUM: 0.7, Confidence.LOW: 0.3}
   134→
   135→# Minimum checks for full dimension weight — below this, weight is dampened
   136→# proportionally. Prevents small-sample dimensions from swinging the overall score.
   137→MIN_SAMPLE = 200
   138→
   139→# Holistic review weight: findings with file="." and detail.holistic=True
   140→# get a 10x weight multiplier for display/priority purposes (issues list,
   141→# remediation engine).  NOT used in score computation — review findings are
   142→# excluded from the detection scoring pipeline (scored via assessments only).
   143→HOLISTIC_MULTIPLIER = 10.0
   144→HOLISTIC_POTENTIAL = 10
   145→
   146→# Budget: subjective dimensions get this fraction of the overall score.
   147→# Mechanical dimensions get the remainder.
   148→SUBJECTIVE_WEIGHT_FRACTION = 0.60
   149→MECHANICAL_WEIGHT_FRACTION = 1.0 - SUBJECTIVE_WEIGHT_FRACTION
   150→
   151→# Per-dimension weighting within the mechanical pool.
   152→# Keep this balanced: no special boost for security/test in the pool itself.
   153→MECHANICAL_DIMENSION_WEIGHTS: dict[str, float] = {
   154→    "file health": 2.0,
   155→    "code quality": 1.0,
   156→    "duplication": 1.0,
   157→    "test health": 1.0,
   158→    "security": 1.0,
   159→}
   160→
   161→# Per-dimension weighting within the subjective pool.
   162→# Emphasize elegance and contract/type coherence for a stronger architecture north star.
   163→SUBJECTIVE_DIMENSION_WEIGHTS: dict[str, float] = {
   164→    "high elegance": 22.0,
   165→    "mid elegance": 22.0,
   166→    "low elegance": 12.0,
   167→    "contracts": 12.0,
   168→    "type safety": 12.0,
   169→    "abstraction fit": 8.0,
   170→    "logic clarity": 6.0,
   171→    # Low-but-meaningful structural signal (about half of the subjective

   173→    "structure nav": 5.0,
   174→    "error consistency": 3.0,
   175→    "naming quality": 2.0,
   176→    "ai generated debt": 1.0,
   177→    "design coherence": 10.0,
   178→}
   179→
   180→# Synthetic check count for subjective dimensions in dimension_scores.

   182→
   183→FAILURE_STATUSES_BY_MODE: dict[ScoreMode, frozenset[str]] = {

   185→    "strict": frozenset({"open", "wontfix"}),
   186→    "verified_strict": frozenset({"open", "wontfix", "fixed", "false_positive"}),
```

```
1→"""State filtering, ignore rules, and finding pattern matching."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import fnmatch
     6→
     7→__all__ = [
     8→    "finding_in_scan_scope",
     9→    "open_scope_breakdown",
    10→    "path_scoped_findings",
    11→    "is_ignored",
    12→    "matched_ignore_pattern",
    13→    "remove_ignored_findings",
    14→    "add_ignore",
    15→    "make_finding",
    16→]
    17→
    18→from desloppify.engine._state.schema import (
    19→    Finding,
    20→    StateModel,
    21→    ensure_state_defaults,
    22→    utc_now,
    23→    validate_state_invariants,
    24→)
    25→from desloppify.core.discovery_api import rel
    26→
    27→
    28→def path_scoped_findings(
    29→    findings: dict[str, Finding],
    30→    scan_path: str | None,
    31→) -> dict[str, Finding]:
    32→    """Filter findings to those within the given scan path."""
    33→    return {
    34→        finding_id: finding
    35→        for finding_id, finding in findings.items()
    36→        if finding_in_scan_scope(str(finding.get("file", "")), scan_path)
    37→    }
    38→
    39→
    40→def finding_in_scan_scope(file_path: str, scan_path: str | None) -> bool:
    41→    """Return True when a file path belongs to the active scan scope."""
    42→    if not scan_path or scan_path == ".":
    43→        return True
    44→    prefix = scan_path.rstrip("/") + "/"
    45→    return (
    46→        file_path.startswith(prefix)
    47→        or file_path == scan_path
    48→        or file_path == "."
    49→    )
    50→
    51→
    52→def open_scope_breakdown(
    53→    findings: dict[str, Finding],
    54→    scan_path: str | None,
    55→    *,
    56→    detector: str | None = None,
    57→) -> dict[str, int]:
    58→    """Return open-finding counts split by in-scope vs out-of-scope carryover."""
    59→    in_scope = 0
    60→    out_of_scope = 0
    61→
    62→    for finding in findings.values():
    63→        if finding.get("status") != "open":
    64→            continue
    65→        if detector is not None and finding.get("detector") != detector:
    66→            continue
    67→        file_path = str(finding.get("file", ""))
    68→        if finding_in_scan_scope(file_path, scan_path):
    69→            in_scope += 1
    70→        else:
    71→            out_of_scope += 1
    72→
    73→    return {
    74→        "in_scope": in_scope,
    75→        "out_of_scope": out_of_scope,
    76→        "global": in_scope + out_of_scope,
    77→    }
    78→
    79→
    80→def is_ignored(finding_id: str, file: str, ignore_patterns: list[str]) -> bool:
    81→    """Check if a finding matches any ignore pattern (glob, ID prefix, or file path)."""
    82→    return matched_ignore_pattern(finding_id, file, ignore_patterns) is not None
    83→
    84→
    85→def matched_ignore_pattern(
    86→    finding_id: str, file: str, ignore_patterns: list[str]
    87→) -> str | None:
    88→    """Return the ignore pattern that matched, if any."""
    89→    for pattern in ignore_patterns:
    90→        if "*" in pattern:
    91→            target = finding_id if "::" in pattern else file
    92→            if fnmatch.fnmatch(target, pattern):
    93→                return pattern
    94→            continue
    95→
    96→        if "::" in pattern:
    97→            if finding_id.startswith(pattern):
    98→                return pattern
    99→            continue
   100→
   101→        if file == pattern or file == rel(pattern):
   102→            return pattern
   103→
   104→    return None
   105→
   106→
   107→def remove_ignored_findings(state: StateModel, pattern: str) -> int:
   108→    """Suppress findings matching an ignore pattern. Returns count affected."""
   109→    ensure_state_defaults(state)
   110→    matched_ids = [
   111→        finding_id
   112→        for finding_id, finding in state["findings"].items()
   113→        if is_ignored(finding_id, finding["file"], [pattern])
   114→    ]
   115→    now = utc_now()
   116→    for finding_id in matched_ids:
   117→        finding = state["findings"][finding_id]
   118→        finding["suppressed"] = True
   119→        finding["suppressed_at"] = now
   120→        finding["suppression_pattern"] = pattern
   121→        if finding.get("status") in ("fixed", "auto_resolved", "false_positive"):
   122→            finding["status"] = "open"
   123→            finding["resolved_at"] = None
   124→            finding["note"] = (
   125→                "Suppressed by ignore pattern — remains unresolved for score integrity"
   126→            )
   127→    # Deferred import to avoid circular dependency with engine._state.scoring
   128→    from desloppify.engine._state.scoring import _recompute_stats
   129→
   130→    _recompute_stats(state, scan_path=state.get("scan_path"))
   131→    validate_state_invariants(state)
   132→    return len(matched_ids)
   133→
   134→
   135→def add_ignore(state: StateModel, pattern: str) -> int:
   136→    """Add an ignore pattern and remove existing matching findings."""
   137→    ensure_state_defaults(state)
   138→    config = state.setdefault("config", {})
   139→    ignores = config.setdefault("ignore", [])
   140→    if pattern not in ignores:
   141→        ignores.append(pattern)
   142→    return remove_ignored_findings(state, pattern)
   143→
   144→
   145→def make_finding(
   146→    detector: str,
   147→    file: str,
   148→    name: str,
   149→    *,
   150→    tier: int,
   151→    confidence: str,
   152→    summary: str,
   153→    detail: dict | None = None,
   154→) -> Finding:
   155→    """Create a normalized finding dict with a stable ID."""
   156→    rfile = rel(file)
   157→    finding_id = f"{detector}::{rfile}::{name}" if name else f"{detector}::{rfile}"
   158→    now = utc_now()
   159→    return {
   160→        "id": finding_id,
   161→        "detector": detector,
   162→        "file": rfile,
   163→        "tier": tier,
   164→        "confidence": confidence,
   165→        "summary": summary,
   166→        "detail": detail or {},
   167→        "status": "open",
   168→        "note": None,
   169→        "first_seen": now,
   170→        "last_seen": now,
   171→        "resolved_at": None,
   172→        "reopen_count": 0,
   173→    }
   174→
   175→
   176→def _matches_pattern(finding_id: str, finding: dict[str, str], pattern: str) -> bool:
   177→    """Check if a finding matches by ID, glob, prefix, detector, or path."""
   178→    return (
   179→        finding_id == pattern
   180→        or ("*" in pattern and fnmatch.fnmatch(finding_id, pattern))
   181→        or ("::" in pattern and finding_id.startswith(pattern))
   182→        or (
   183→            finding.get("detector") == pattern
   184→            or finding["file"] == pattern
   185→            or finding["file"].startswith(pattern.rstrip("/") + "/")
   186→        )
   187→    )
   188→
```

```
   123→            "files_fixed": len(results),
   124→            "items_fixed": total_items,
   125→            "findings_resolved": len(resolved_ids),
   126→            "overall_score": new.overall,
   127→            "objective_score": new.objective,
   128→            "strict_score": new.strict,
   129→            "prev_overall_score": prev.overall,
   130→            "prev_objective_score": prev.objective,
   131→            "prev_strict_score": prev.strict,
   132→            "skip_reasons": skip_reasons,
   133→            "next_action": next_action,
   134→            "narrative": narrative,
   135→        }
   136→    )
   137→    _print_fix_retro(
   138→        fixer_name, len(entries), total_items, len(resolved_ids), skip_reasons
   139→    )
   140→
   141→
   142→def _report_dry_run(
   143→    args: argparse.Namespace,
   144→    fixer_name: str,
   145→    entries: list[dict],
   146→    results: list[dict],
   147→    total_items: int,
   148→) -> None:
   149→    runtime = command_runtime(args)
   150→    fix_lang = resolve_lang(args)
   151→    fix_lang_name = fix_lang.name if fix_lang else None
   152→    state = runtime.state
   153→    narrative = narrative_mod.compute_narrative(
   154→        state,
   155→        context=narrative_mod.NarrativeContext(lang=fix_lang_name, command="fix"),
   156→    )
   157→    write_query(
   158→        {
   159→            "command": "fix",
   160→            "fixer": fixer_name,
   161→            "dry_run": True,
   162→            "files_would_fix": len(results),
   163→            "items_would_fix": total_items,
   164→            "narrative": narrative,
   165→        }
   166→    )
   167→    skipped = len(entries) - total_items
   168→    if skipped > 0:
   169→        print(colorize("\n  ── Review ──", "dim"))
   170→        print(
   171→            colorize(
   172→                f"  {total_items} of {len(entries)} entries would be fixed ({skipped} skipped).",
   173→                "dim",
   174→            )
   175→        )
   176→        for q in [
   177→            "Do the sample changes look correct? Any false positives?",
   178→            "Are the skipped items truly unfixable, or could the fixer be improved?",
   179→            "Ready to run without --dry-run? (git push first!)",
   180→        ]:
   181→            print(colorize(f"  - {q}", "dim"))
   182→
   183→
   184→def _resolve_fixer_results(
   185→    state: dict, results: list[dict], detector: str, fixer_name: str
   186→) -> list[str]:
   187→    resolved_ids = []
   188→    for r in results:
   189→        rfile = rel(r["file"])
   190→        for sym in r["removed"]:
   191→            fid = f"{detector}::{rfile}::{sym}"
   192→            if fid in state["findings"] and state["findings"][fid]["status"] == "open":
   193→                state["findings"][fid]["status"] = "fixed"
   194→                state["findings"][fid]["note"] = (
   195→                    f"auto-fixed by desloppify fix {fixer_name}"
   196→                )
   197→                resolved_ids.append(fid)
   198→    return resolved_ids
   199→
   200→
   201→def _warn_uncommitted_changes() -> None:
   202→    try:
   203→        r = subprocess.run(
   204→            ["git", "status", "--porcelain"], capture_output=True, text=True, timeout=5
   205→        )
   206→        if r.stdout.strip():
   207→            print(
   208→                colorize(
   209→                    "\n  ⚠ You have uncommitted changes. Consider running:", "yellow"
   210→                )
   211→            )
   212→            print(
   213→                colorize(
   214→                    "    git add -A && git commit -m 'pre-fix checkpoint' && git push",
   215→                    "yellow",
   216→                )
   217→            )
   218→            print(
   219→                colorize(
   220→                    "    This ensures you can revert if the fixer produces unexpected results.\n",
   221→                    "dim",
   222→                )
   223→            )
   224→    except (OSError, subprocess.TimeoutExpired):
   225→        return
   226→
   227→
   228→def _cascade_unused_import_cleanup(
   229→    path: Path,
   230→    state: dict,
   231→    _prev_score: float,
   232→    dry_run: bool,
   233→    *,
   234→    lang: LangRun | None = None,
   235→) -> None:
   236→    if not lang or "unused-imports" not in getattr(lang, "fixers", {}):
   237→        print(colorize("  Cascade: no unused-imports fixer for this language", "dim"))
   238→        return
   239→
   240→    fixer = lang.fixers["unused-imports"]
   241→    print(colorize("\n  Running cascading import cleanup...", "dim"), file=sys.stderr)
   242→    entries = fixer.detect(path)
   243→    if not entries:
   244→        print(colorize("  Cascade: no orphaned imports found", "dim"))
   245→        return
   246→
   247→    raw = fixer.fix(entries, dry_run=dry_run)
   248→    if isinstance(raw, FixResult):
   249→        results = raw.entries
   250→    else:
   251→        results = raw
   252→
   253→    if not results:
   254→        print(colorize("  Cascade: no orphaned imports found", "dim"))
   255→        return
   256→    n_removed = sum(len(r["removed"]) for r in results)
   257→    n_lines = sum(r["lines_removed"] for r in results)
   258→    print(
   259→        colorize(
   260→            f"  Cascade: removed {n_removed} now-orphaned imports "
   261→            f"from {len(results)} files ({n_lines} lines)",
   262→            "green",
   263→        )
   264→    )
   265→    resolved = _resolve_fixer_results(
   266→        state, results, fixer.detector, "cascade-unused-imports"
   267→    )
   268→    if resolved:
   269→        print(f"  Cascade: auto-resolved {len(resolved)} import findings")
   270→
   271→_COMMAND_POST_FIX["debug-logs"] = _cascade_unused_import_cleanup
   272→_COMMAND_POST_FIX["dead-useeffect"] = _cascade_unused_import_cleanup
   273→
   274→_SKIP_REASON_LABELS = {
   275→    "rest_element": "has ...rest (removing changes rest contents)",
   276→    "array_destructuring": "array destructuring (positional — can't remove)",
   277→    "function_param": "function/callback parameter (use `fix unused-params` to prefix with _)",
   278→    "standalone_var_with_call": "standalone variable with function call (may have side effects)",
   279→    "no_destr_context": "destructuring member without context",
   280→    "out_of_range": "line out of range (stale data?)",
   281→    "other": "other patterns (needs manual review)",
   282→}
   283→
   284→
   285→def _print_fix_retro(
   286→    fixer_name: str,
   287→    detected: int,
   288→    fixed: int,
   289→    resolved: int,
   290→    skip_reasons: dict[str, int] | None = None,
   291→):
   292→    skipped = detected - fixed
   293→    print(colorize("\n  ── Post-fix check ──", "dim"))
   294→    print(
   295→        colorize(
   296→            f"  Fixed {fixed}/{detected} ({skipped} skipped, {resolved} findings resolved)",
   297→            "dim",
   298→        )
   299→    )
   300→    if skip_reasons and skipped > 0:
   301→        print(colorize(f"\n  Skip reasons ({skipped} total):", "dim"))
   302→        for reason, count in sorted(skip_reasons.items(), key=lambda x: -x[1]):
   303→            print(
   304→                colorize(
   305→                    f"    {count:4d}  {_SKIP_REASON_LABELS.get(reason, reason)}", "dim"
   306→                )
   307→            )
   308→        print()
   309→    checklist = [
   310→        "Run your language typecheck/build command — does it still build?",
   311→        "Spot-check a few changed files — do the edits look correct?",
   312→    ]
   313→    if skipped > 0 and not skip_reasons:
   314→        checklist.append(
   315→            f"{skipped} items were skipped. Should the fixer handle more patterns?"
   316→        )
   317→    checklist += [
   318→        "Run `desloppify scan` to update state. Did score improve as expected?",
   319→        "Are there cascading effects? (e.g., removing vars may orphan imports)",
   320→        "`git diff --stat` — review before committing. Anything surprising?",
   321→    ]
   322→    print(colorize("  Checklist:", "dim"))
   323→    for i, item in enumerate(checklist, 1):
   324→        print(colorize(f"  {i}. {item}", "dim"))
   325→
```

```
    18→def _enable_live_review_output() -> None:
    19→    """Best-effort: force line-buffered review output for non-TTY runners."""
    20→    for stream_name in ("stdout", "stderr"):
    21→        stream = getattr(sys, stream_name, None)
    22→        reconfigure = getattr(stream, "reconfigure", None)
    23→        if not callable(reconfigure):
    24→            continue
    25→        try:
    26→            reconfigure(line_buffering=True, write_through=True)
    27→        except (OSError, ValueError, TypeError):
    28→            continue
    29→
    30→
    31→def cmd_review(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
```

```
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations

    13→_COMMAND_POST_FIX: dict[str, Callable[..., None]] = {}

    15→
    16→def _load_fixer(args, fixer_name: str) -> tuple[LangConfig, FixerConfig]:
    17→    """Resolve fixer from language plugin registry, or exit."""
    18→    lang = resolve_lang(args)
    19→    if not lang:
    20→        print(colorize("Could not detect language. Use --lang to specify.", "red"), file=sys.stderr)
    21→        sys.exit(1)
    22→    if not lang.fixers:
    23→        print(colorize(f"No auto-fixers available for {lang.name}.", "red"), file=sys.stderr)
    24→        sys.exit(1)
    25→    if fixer_name not in lang.fixers:
    26→        available = ", ".join(sorted(lang.fixers.keys()))
    27→        print(colorize(f"Unknown fixer: {fixer_name}", "red"), file=sys.stderr)
    28→        print(colorize(f"  Available: {available}", "dim"), file=sys.stderr)
    29→        sys.exit(1)
    30→    fc = lang.fixers[fixer_name]
    31→    if fixer_name in _COMMAND_POST_FIX and not fc.post_fix:
    32→        fc = dataclasses.replace(fc, post_fix=_COMMAND_POST_FIX[fixer_name])
    33→    return lang, fc
    34→
```

```
    37→class ImportFlagValidationError(ValueError):
    38→    """Raised when review import CLI flags are mutually incompatible."""
    39→
    40→
    41→def _validate_import_flag_combos(
    42→    *,
    43→    attested_external: bool,
    44→    allow_partial: bool,
    45→    override_enabled: bool,
    46→    override_attest: str | None,
    47→) -> None:
    48→    """Fail fast on conflicting import flags to keep behavior explicit."""
    49→    if attested_external and override_enabled:
    50→        raise ImportFlagValidationError(
    51→            "--attested-external cannot be combined with --manual-override"
    52→        )
    53→    if attested_external and allow_partial:
    54→        raise ImportFlagValidationError(
    55→            "--attested-external cannot be combined with --allow-partial"
    56→        )
    57→    if override_enabled and allow_partial:
    58→        raise ImportFlagValidationError(
    59→            "--manual-override cannot be combined with --allow-partial"
    60→        )
    61→    if override_enabled and (
    62→        not isinstance(override_attest, str) or not override_attest.strip()
    63→    ):
    64→        raise ImportFlagValidationError("--manual-override requires --attest")
    65→
    66→
    67→def _imported_assessment_keys(findings_data: ReviewImportPayload) -> set[str]:
    68→    """Return normalized assessment dimension keys from payload."""
    69→    raw_assessments = findings_data["assessments"]
    70→    keys: set[str] = set()
    71→    for raw_key in raw_assessments:
    72→        normalized = normalize_dimension_name(str(raw_key))
    73→        if normalized:
    74→            keys.add(normalized)
    75→    return keys
    76→
    77→
    78→def _mark_manual_override_assessments_provisional(
    79→    state: dict,
    80→    *,
    81→    assessment_keys: set[str],
    82→) -> int:
    83→    """Mark imported manual override assessments as provisional until next scan."""
    84→    if not assessment_keys:
    85→        return 0
    86→    store = state.get("subjective_assessments")
    87→    if not isinstance(store, dict):
    88→        return 0
    89→
    90→    now = state_mod.utc_now()
    91→    expires_scan = int(state.get("scan_count", 0) or 0) + 1
    92→    marked = 0
    93→    for key in sorted(assessment_keys):
    94→        payload = store.get(key)
    95→        if not isinstance(payload, dict):
    96→            continue
    97→        payload["source"] = "manual_override"
    98→        payload["assessed_at"] = now
    99→        payload["provisional_override"] = True
   100→        payload["provisional_until_scan"] = expires_scan
```

```
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import json
     6→import logging
     7→import shutil
     8→import sys
     9→from pathlib import Path
    10→from typing import cast
    11→
    12→__all__ = [
    13→    "load_state",
    14→    "save_state",
    15→]
    16→
    17→from desloppify.core._internal.text_utils import is_numeric
    18→from desloppify.engine._state.schema import (
    19→    CURRENT_VERSION,
    20→    STATE_FILE,
    21→    StateModel,
    22→    empty_state,
    23→    ensure_state_defaults,
    24→    json_default,
    25→    validate_state_invariants,
    26→)
    27→from desloppify.engine._state.scoring import _recompute_stats
    28→from desloppify.core.discovery_api import safe_write_text
    29→
    30→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    31→
    32→
    33→def _load_json(path: Path) -> dict[str, object]:
    34→    data = json.loads(path.read_text())
    35→    if not isinstance(data, dict):
    36→        raise ValueError("state file root must be a JSON object")
    37→    return data
    38→
    39→
    40→def _normalize_loaded_state(data: object) -> dict[str, object]:
    41→    if not isinstance(data, dict):
    42→        raise ValueError("state file root must be a JSON object")
    43→    ensure_state_defaults(data)
    44→    normalized = cast(StateModel, data)
    45→    validate_state_invariants(normalized)
    46→    return normalized
    47→
    48→
    49→def load_state(path: Path | None = None) -> StateModel:
    50→    """Load state from disk, or return empty state on missing/corruption."""
    51→    state_path = path or STATE_FILE
    52→    if not state_path.exists():
    53→        return empty_state()
    54→
    55→    try:
    56→        data = _load_json(state_path)
    57→    except (json.JSONDecodeError, UnicodeDecodeError, OSError, ValueError) as ex:
    58→        backup = state_path.with_suffix(".json.bak")
    59→        if backup.exists():
    60→            try:
    61→                backup_data = _load_json(backup)
    62→                print(
    63→                    f"  ⚠ State file corrupted ({ex}), loaded from backup.",
    64→                    file=sys.stderr,
    65→                )
    66→                return _normalize_loaded_state(backup_data)
    67→            except (
    68→                json.JSONDecodeError,
    69→                UnicodeDecodeError,
    70→                OSError,
    71→                ValueError,
    72→                TypeError,
    73→                AttributeError,
    74→            ) as backup_ex:
    75→                logger.debug("Backup state load failed from %s: %s", backup, backup_ex)
    76→
    77→        print(f"  ⚠ State file corrupted ({ex}). Starting fresh.", file=sys.stderr)
    78→        rename_failed = False
    79→        try:
    80→            state_path.rename(state_path.with_suffix(".json.corrupted"))
```

```
1→"""Core query payload writing helpers."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import json
     6→import logging
     7→import sys
     8→from pathlib import Path
     9→
    10→from desloppify.core.config import config_for_query, load_config
    11→from desloppify.core.file_paths import safe_write_text
    12→from desloppify.core.output_contract import OutputResult
    13→from desloppify.state import json_default
    14→
    15→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    16→
    17→
    18→def write_query(data: dict, *, query_file: Path) -> OutputResult:
    19→    """Write structured query payloads with config context and graceful fallback."""
    20→    if "config" not in data:
    21→        try:
    22→            data["config"] = config_for_query(load_config())
    23→        except (OSError, ValueError, json.JSONDecodeError) as exc:
    24→            data["config_error"] = str(exc)
    25→            logger.debug("Skipping config injection into query payload: %s", exc)
    26→    try:
    27→        safe_write_text(
    28→            query_file, json.dumps(data, indent=2, default=json_default) + "\n"
    29→        )
    30→        print("  → query.json updated", file=sys.stderr)
    31→        return OutputResult(
    32→            ok=True,
    33→            status="written",
    34→            message=f"query payload written to {query_file}",
    35→        )
    36→    except OSError as exc:
    37→        data["query_write_error"] = str(exc)
    38→        print(f"  ⚠ Could not write query.json: {exc}", file=sys.stderr)
    39→        return OutputResult(
    40→            ok=False,
    41→            status="error",
    42→            message=str(exc),
    43→            error_kind="query_write_error",
    44→        )
    45→
    46→
    47→__all__ = ["write_query"]
    48→
```

```
1→"""zone command: show/set/clear zone classifications."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import argparse
     6→import sys
     7→from pathlib import Path
     8→
     9→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.lang import resolve_lang
    10→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.rendering import print_agent_plan
    11→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.runtime import command_runtime
    12→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.state import state_path
    13→from desloppify.core import config as config_mod
    14→from desloppify.core.fallbacks import print_error
    15→from desloppify.engine.policy.zones import FileZoneMap, Zone
    16→from desloppify.core.discovery_api import rel
    17→from desloppify.core.output_api import colorize
    18→
    19→
    20→def cmd_zone(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
    21→    """Handle zone subcommands: show, set, clear."""
    22→    action = getattr(args, "zone_action", None)
    23→    if action in (None, "show"):
    24→        _zone_show(args)
    25→    elif action == "set":
    26→        _zone_set(args)
    27→    elif action == "clear":
    28→        _zone_clear(args)
    29→    else:
    30→        print(colorize("Usage: desloppify zone {show|set|clear}", "red"), file=sys.stderr)
    31→        sys.exit(1)
    32→
    33→
    34→def _zone_show(args):
    35→    """Show zone classifications for all scanned files."""
    36→    state_file = state_path(args)
    37→    if not state_file.exists():
    38→        print(colorize("No state file found — run a scan first.", "red"), file=sys.stderr)
    39→        sys.exit(1)
    40→    lang = resolve_lang(args)
    41→    if not lang or not lang.file_finder:
    42→        print(colorize("No language detected — run a scan first.", "red"), file=sys.stderr)
    43→        sys.exit(1)
    44→
    45→    path = Path(args.path)
    46→    overrides = command_runtime(args).config.get("zone_overrides", {})
    47→
    48→    files = lang.file_finder(path)
    49→    zone_map = FileZoneMap(
    50→        files, lang.zone_rules, rel_fn=rel, overrides=overrides or None
    51→    )
    52→
    53→    # Group files by zone
    54→    by_zone: dict[str, list[str]] = {}
    55→    for f in sorted(files, key=lambda f: rel(f)):
    56→        zone = zone_map.get(f)
    57→        by_zone.setdefault(zone.value, []).append(f)
    58→
    59→    total = len(files)
    60→    print(colorize(f"\nZone classifications ({total} files)\n", "bold"))
    61→
    62→    for zone_val in ["production", "test", "config", "generated", "script", "vendor"]:
    63→        zone_files = by_zone.get(zone_val, [])
    64→        if not zone_files:
    65→            continue
    66→        print(colorize(f"  {zone_val} ({len(zone_files)} files)", "bold"))
    67→        for f in zone_files:
    68→            rp = rel(f)
    69→            is_override = rp in overrides
    70→            suffix = colorize(" (override)", "cyan") if is_override else ""
    71→            print(f"    {rp}{suffix}")
    72→        print()
    73→
    74→    if overrides:
    75→        print(colorize(f"  {len(overrides)} override(s) active", "dim"))
    76→    print(colorize("  Override: desloppify zone set <file> <zone>", "dim"))
    77→    print(colorize("  Clear:    desloppify zone clear <file>", "dim"))
    78→    print_agent_plan(
    79→        ["Fix misclassified files, then re-scan."],
    80→        next_command="desloppify scan",
    81→    )
    82→
    83→
    84→def _zone_set(args):
    85→    """Set a zone override for a file."""
    86→    filepath = args.zone_path
    87→    zone_value = args.zone_value
    88→
    89→    # Validate zone value
    90→    valid_zones = {z.value for z in Zone}
    91→    if zone_value not in valid_zones:
    92→        print(
    93→            colorize(
    94→                f"Invalid zone: {zone_value}. Valid: {', '.join(sorted(valid_zones))}",
    95→                "red",
    96→            ),
    97→            file=sys.stderr,
    98→        )
    99→        sys.exit(1)
   100→
   101→    normalized = rel(filepath)
   102→    config = command_runtime(args).config
   103→    config.setdefault("zone_overrides", {})[normalized] = zone_value
   104→    try:
   105→        config_mod.save_config(config)
   106→    except OSError as e:
   107→        print_error(f"could not save config: {e}")
   108→        sys.exit(1)
   109→    print(f"  Set {normalized} → {zone_value}")
   110→    print(colorize("  Run `desloppify scan` to apply.", "dim"))
   111→    print(colorize("  Next command: `desloppify scan`", "dim"))
   112→
   113→
   114→def _zone_clear(args):
   115→    """Clear a zone override for a file."""
   116→    filepath = args.zone_path
   117→
   118→    normalized = rel(filepath)
   119→    config = command_runtime(args).config
   120→    overrides = config.get("zone_overrides", {})
   121→    if normalized in overrides:
   122→        del overrides[normalized]
   123→        try:
   124→            config_mod.save_config(config)
   125→        except OSError as e:
   126→            print_error(f"could not save config: {e}")
   127→            sys.exit(1)
   128→        print(f"  Cleared override for {normalized}")
   129→        print(colorize("  Run `desloppify scan` to apply.", "dim"))
   130→        print(colorize("  Next command: `desloppify scan`", "dim"))
   131→    else:
   132→        print(colorize(f"  No override found for {normalized}", "yellow"))
   133→
```

```
150→    overall_score: Required[float]
   151→    objective_score: Required[float]

   159→    subjective_integrity: Required[SubjectiveIntegrity]

   164→class ScanDiff(TypedDict):
   165→    new: int
   166→    auto_resolved: int
   167→    reopened: int
   168→    total_current: int
   169→    suspect_detectors: list[str]
   170→    chronic_reopeners: list[dict]
   171→    skipped_other_lang: int
   172→    skipped_out_of_scope: int
   173→    ignored: int
   174→    ignore_patterns: int
   175→    raw_findings: int
   176→    suppressed_pct: float
   177→    skipped: NotRequired[int]
   178→    skipped_details: NotRequired[list[dict]]
   179→
   180→
   181→STATE_DIR = PROJECT_ROOT / ".desloppify"
   182→STATE_FILE = STATE_DIR / "state.json"
   183→CURRENT_VERSION = 1
   184→
   185→
   186→def utc_now() -> str:
   187→    """Return current UTC timestamp with second-level precision."""
   188→    return datetime.now(UTC).isoformat(timespec="seconds")
   189→
   190→
   191→def empty_state() -> StateModel:
   192→    """Return a new empty state payload."""
   193→    return {
   194→        "version": CURRENT_VERSION,
   195→        "created": utc_now(),
   196→        "last_scan": None,
   197→        "scan_count": 0,
   198→        "overall_score": 0,
   199→        "objective_score": 0,
   200→        "strict_score": 0,
   201→        "verified_strict_score": 0,
   202→        "stats": {},
   203→        "findings": {},
   204→        "scan_coverage": {},
   205→        "score_confidence": {},
   206→        "subjective_integrity": {},
   207→        "subjective_assessments": {},
   208→    }
   209→
   210→
   211→def _as_non_negative_int(value: Any, default: int = 0) -> int:
   212→    try:
   213→        parsed = int(value)
   214→    except (TypeError, ValueError):
   215→        return default
   216→    return parsed if parsed >= 0 else 0
   217→
   218→
   219→def ensure_state_defaults(state: StateModel | dict) -> None:
   220→    """Normalize loose/legacy state payloads to a valid base shape in-place."""
   221→    for key, value in empty_state().items():
   222→        state.setdefault(key, value)
   223→
   224→    if not isinstance(state.get("findings"), dict):
   225→        state["findings"] = {}
   226→    if not isinstance(state.get("stats"), dict):
   227→        state["stats"] = {}
   228→    if not isinstance(state.get("scan_history"), list):
   229→        state["scan_history"] = []
   230→    if not isinstance(state.get("scan_coverage"), dict):
   231→        state["scan_coverage"] = {}
   232→    if not isinstance(state.get("score_confidence"), dict):
   233→        state["score_confidence"] = {}
   234→    if not isinstance(state.get("subjective_integrity"), dict):
   235→        state["subjective_integrity"] = {}
   236→
   237→    findings = state["findings"]
   238→    to_remove: list[str] = []
   239→    for finding_id, finding in findings.items():
   240→        if not isinstance(finding, dict):
   241→            to_remove.append(finding_id)
   242→            continue
   243→
   244→        finding.setdefault("id", finding_id)
   245→        finding.setdefault("detector", "unknown")
   246→        finding.setdefault("file", "")
   247→        finding.setdefault("tier", 3)
   248→        finding.setdefault("confidence", "low")
   249→        finding.setdefault("summary", "")
   250→        finding.setdefault("detail", {})
   251→        finding.setdefault("status", "open")
   252→        finding["status"] = canonical_finding_status(
   253→            finding.get("status"),
   254→            default="open",
   255→        )
   256→        finding.setdefault("note", None)
   257→        finding.setdefault("first_seen", state.get("created") or utc_now())
   258→        finding.setdefault("last_seen", finding["first_seen"])
   259→        finding.setdefault("resolved_at", None)
   260→        finding["reopen_count"] = _as_non_negative_int(
   261→            finding.get("reopen_count", 0), default=0
   262→        )
   263→        finding.setdefault("suppressed", False)
   264→        finding.setdefault("suppressed_at", None)
   265→        finding.setdefault("suppression_pattern", None)
   266→
   267→    for finding_id in to_remove:
   268→        findings.pop(finding_id, None)
   269→
   270→    for entry in state["scan_history"]:
   271→        if not isinstance(entry, dict):
   272→            continue
   273→        integrity = entry.get("subjective_integrity")
   274→        if integrity is not None and not isinstance(integrity, dict):
   275→            entry["subjective_integrity"] = None
   276→
   277→    state["scan_count"] = _as_non_negative_int(state.get("scan_count", 0), default=0)
   278→    return None
   279→
   280→
   281→def validate_state_invariants(state: StateModel) -> None:
   282→    """Raise ValueError when core state invariants are violated."""
   283→    if not isinstance(state.get("findings"), dict):
   284→        raise ValueError("state.findings must be a dict")
   285→    if not isinstance(state.get("stats"), dict):
   286→        raise ValueError("state.stats must be a dict")
   287→
   288→    findings = state["findings"]
   289→    for finding_id, finding in findings.items():
   290→        if not isinstance(finding, dict):
   291→            raise ValueError(f"finding {finding_id!r} must be a dict")
   292→        if finding.get("id") != finding_id:
   293→            raise ValueError(f"finding id mismatch for {finding_id!r}")
   294→        if finding.get("status") not in _ALLOWED_FINDING_STATUSES:
   295→            raise ValueError(
   296→                f"finding {finding_id!r} has invalid status {finding.get('status')!r}"
   297→            )
   298→
   299→        tier = finding.get("tier")
   300→        if not isinstance(tier, int) or tier < 1 or tier > 4:
   301→            raise ValueError(f"finding {finding_id!r} has invalid tier {tier!r}")
   302→
   303→        reopen_count = finding.get("reopen_count")
   304→        if not isinstance(reopen_count, int) or reopen_count < 0:
   305→            raise ValueError(
   306→                f"finding {finding_id!r} has invalid reopen_count {reopen_count!r}"
   307→            )
   308→
   309→
   310→def json_default(obj: Any) -> Any:
   311→    """JSON serializer that handles known types and rejects unknowns."""
   312→    if isinstance(obj, set):
   313→        return sorted(obj)
   314→    if isinstance(obj, Path):
   315→        return str(obj).replace("\\", "/")
   316→    if hasattr(obj, "isoformat"):
   317→        return obj.isoformat()
   318→    raise TypeError(
   319→        f"Object of type {type(obj).__name__} is not JSON serializable: {obj!r}"
   320→    )
   321→
   322→
   323→def get_overall_score(state: StateModel) -> float | None:
   324→    """Canonical overall score (lenient, includes subjective dimensions)."""
   325→    return state.get("overall_score")
   326→
   327→
   328→def get_objective_score(state: StateModel) -> float | None:
   329→    """Canonical objective score (lenient, mechanical dimensions only)."""
   330→    return state.get("objective_score")
   331→
   332→
   333→def get_strict_score(state: StateModel) -> float | None:
   334→    """Canonical strict score (strict, includes subjective dimensions)."""
   335→    return state.get("strict_score")
   336→
   337→
   338→def get_verified_strict_score(state: StateModel) -> float | None:
   339→    """Strict score that only credits scan-verified fixes.
   340→
   341→    Returns None if no scan-verified score exists yet (fresh state or
   342→    no scan has run). Does not fall back to the unverified strict_score.
   343→    """
   344→    return state.get("verified_strict_score")
   345→
```

```
    34→def _show_concerns(state: dict, lang_name: str | None) -> None:
    35→    """Render current design concerns from mechanical signals."""
    36→    from desloppify.engine.concerns import generate_concerns
    37→
    38→    concerns = generate_concerns(state, lang_name=lang_name)
    39→    if not concerns:
    40→        print(colorize("  No design concerns detected.", "green"))
    41→        return
    42→
    43→    print(colorize(f"\n  {len(concerns)} design concern(s):\n", "bold"))
    44→    dismissals = state.get("concern_dismissals", {})
    45→
    46→    for i, c in enumerate(concerns, 1):
    47→        print(colorize(f"  {i}. [{c.type}] {c.file}", "cyan"))
    48→        print(f"     {c.summary}")
    49→        for ev in c.evidence:
    50→            print(colorize(f"       - {ev}", "dim"))
    51→        print(colorize(f"     ? {c.question}", "yellow"))
    52→
    53→        # Check if previously dismissed (but resurface due to changed findings).
    54→        prev = dismissals.get(c.fingerprint)
    55→        if isinstance(prev, dict):
    56→            reasoning = prev.get("reasoning", "")
    57→            if reasoning:
    58→                print(colorize(f"     (previously dismissed: {reasoning})", "dim"))
    59→        print()
    60→
    61→
    62→def cmd_show(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
    63→    """Show all findings for a file, directory, detector, or pattern."""
    64→    runtime = command_runtime(args)
    65→    state = runtime.state
    66→    config = runtime.config
    67→
    68→    if not require_completed_scan(state):
    69→        return
    70→
    71→    stale_warning = check_tool_staleness(state)
    72→    if stale_warning:
    73→        print(colorize(f"  {stale_warning}", "yellow"))
    74→    skill_warning = check_skill_version()
    75→    if skill_warning:
    76→        print(colorize(f"  {skill_warning}", "yellow"))
    77→
    78→    # Handle "show concerns" as a special view.
    79→    pattern_raw = getattr(args, "pattern", "")
    80→    if pattern_raw and pattern_raw.strip().lower() == "concerns":
    81→        lang = resolve_lang(args)
    82→        _show_concerns(state, lang.name if lang else None)
    83→        return
```

Perfect! Now let me create a comprehensive summary document. Since I'm in read-only mode, I'll provide the findings as a direct response:

## Summary: Commands That Mutate Scoring State Without Recomputing/Reporting Scores

Based on my comprehensive analysis of the codebase, here are all the commands and operations that change scoring state but DON'T currently recompute scores or report them:

### 1. **RESOLVE COMMAND** (`/resolve` / `cmd_resolve`)
**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/resolve/cmd.py`

**Status Mutation Points:**
- `resolve_findings()` in `resolution.py` (line 97-170) — resolves findings by status
- **Score Recomputation:** ✅ YES — calls `_recompute_stats()` at line 168
- **Score Reporting:** ✅ YES — calls `_print_score_movement()` at line 98-106
- **State Save:** ✅ YES — line 87

**Verdict:** FULL COMPLIANCE ✓

---

### 2. **IGNORE PATTERN COMMAND** (`/resolve --ignore-pattern`)
**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/resolve/cmd.py` (line 141-203)

**Status Mutation Points:**
- `remove_ignored_findings()` in `filtering.py` (line 107-132) — suppresses matching findings
- **Score Recomputation:** ✅ YES — calls `_recompute_stats()` at line 130
- **Score Reporting:** ✅ YES — prints scores at lines 170-182
- **State Save:** ✅ YES — line 165

**Verdict:** FULL COMPLIANCE ✓

---

### 3. **FIX COMMAND (Apply Phase)** (`/fix <fixer>`)
**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/fix/apply_flow.py`

**Status Mutation Points:**
- `_resolve_fixer_results()` (line 184-198) — marks detected items as "fixed"
- Modifies state at lines 193-196: `state["findings"][fid]["status"] = "fixed"`
- **Score Recomputation:** ❌ **NO** — After `state_mod.save_state()` at line 81, scores ARE recomputed (line 139 in persistence.py calls `_recompute_stats`)
- **Score Reporting:** ✅ YES — prints before/after scores at lines 84-98
- **State Save:** ✅ YES — line 81

**Verdict:** FULL COMPLIANCE ✓ (recomputed via `save_state()`)

## Auto-Refresh Trigger Points

The following operations change scoring state and would benefit from score delta reporting:

1. **`review --import`** — Missing overall/objective/strict/verified score delta display
2. **`review --merge`** — Missing score reporting entirely
3. **`review --external-submit`** — Delegates to import, inherits missing reporting
4. **`review --run-batches`** — Delegates to import, missing score delta display

All of these already recompute scores (via `save_state()` → `_recompute_stats()`), but they don't show the user before/after score comparison like `resolve`, `fix`, and `scan` do.

## Scoring Architecture Report

Based on my exploration of the Desloppify codebase, here's a complete breakdown of how scoring works—from computation to persistence to reporting.

---

### 1. **Core Score Recomputation: `_recompute_stats()`**

**Location:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_state/scoring.py`, lines 389–411

**Signature:**
```python
def _recompute_stats(
    state: StateModel,
    scan_path: str | None = None,
    *,
    subjective_integrity_target: float | None = None,
) -> None:
```

**What it does:**
- **Main orchestrator** for all scoring computations after finding changes
- Calls `_count_findings()` to tally findings by status (open, fixed, auto_resolved, wontfix, false_positive) and by tier (1–4)
- Updates `state["stats"]` with summary counts
- Calls `_update_objective_health()` to compute the four canonical scores and dimension breakdowns
- Optional anti-gaming penalty enforcement via `subjective_integrity_target`

**Key fields written to state:**
- `state["stats"]` — counters and tier breakdowns
- `state["dimension_scores"]` — per-dimension health (score, strict_score, verified_strict_score, checks, issues, tier, detectors)
- `state["overall_score"]`, `state["objective_score"]`, `state["strict_score"]`, `state["verified_strict_score"]` — the four canonical scores
- `state["subjective_integrity"]` — anti-gaming metadata
- `state["score_confidence"]` — scan coverage impact on score reliability

---

### 2. **Callers of `_recompute_stats()`**

`_recompute_stats()` is invoked at four critical points:

#### a. **After Scan Merge** (`merge.py`, line 180)
```python
# desloppify/engine/_state/merge.py:180
_recompute_stats(
    state,
    scan_path=resolved_options.scan_path,
    subjective_integrity_target=resolved_options.subjective_integrity_target,
)
```
- **Context:** After merging fresh detector findings into persistent state
- **Trigger:** New/updated findings alter the stats and dimension scores

#### b. **On State Persistence** (`persistence.py`, line 139)
```python
# desloppify/engine/_state/persistence.py:139
def save_state(...) -> None:
    _recompute_stats(
        state,
        scan_path=state.get("scan_path"),
        subjective_integrity_target=_resolve_integrity_target(state, subjective_integrity_target),
    )
```
- **Context:** Before atomically writing state to disk
- **Trigger:** Called whenever `save_state()` is invoked (command save points, external API calls)
- **Atomic write pattern:** Temp file + `os.replace()` with `.json.bak` backup

#### c. **After Manual Resolution** (`resolution.py`, line 168)
```python
# desloppify/engine/_state/resolution.py:168
_recompute_stats(state, scan_path=state.get("scan_path"))
```
- **Context:** After user manually marks findings as fixed/wontfix/false_positive
- **Trigger:** `resolve_findings()` updates finding statuses

#### d. **When Adding Ignores** (`filtering.py`, line 130)
```python
# desloppify/engine/_state/filtering.py:130
_recompute_stats(state, scan_path=state.get("scan_path"))
```
- **Context:** After applying ignore patterns to suppress findings
- **Trigger:** `add_ignore()` suppresses findings from score calculation

---

### 3. **Scoring Model: Dimension-Based Objective Scoring**

**Location:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/scoring.py` (facade) and `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_scoring/`

**The four canonical scores (all 0–100):**

1. **`overall_score`** — Lenient, includes subjective dimensions
   - Mechanical (40%) + Subjective (60%) blend
   - Mechanical: auto_resolved counts as fixed
   - Subjective: dimension assessments from holistic review
   
2. **`objective_score`** — Lenient, mechanical dimensions only
   - Excludes subjective_assessment detector
   - Pure detector-derived health
   
3. **`strict_score`** — Strict, includes subjective dimensions
   - Mechanical: auto_resolved counts as open
   - Subjective: same as overall_score
   
4. **`verified_strict_score`** — Strict + scan-verified fixes only
   - Only counts fixes present in the latest scan
   - Prevents gaming via manual resolutions

**Scoring pipeline** (`compute_score_bundle()` in `results/core.py`):
```python
1. compute_dimension_scores_by_mode()
   ↓
2. For each dimension: (potential_checks - weighted_failures) / potential_checks * 100
   ↓
3. Append subjective dimensions (from assessments)
   ↓
4. compute_health_score() 
   ↓
   - Pool-weighted average: mechanical (40%) + subjective (60%)
   - Per-dimension weight · sample factor (min(checks/200, 1.0))
   - Result: overall_score
```

---

### 4. **State Persistence: `save_state()`**

**Location:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_state/persistence.py`, lines 131–170

**Signature:**
```python
def save_state(
    state: StateModel,
    path: Path | None = None,
    *,
    subjective_integrity_target: float | None = None,
) -> None:
```

**What gets written:**
- Entire `StateModel` dict serialized to JSON
- Key scored fields:
  - `overall_score`, `objective_score`, `strict_score`, `verified_strict_score`
  - `stats` (counters by status and tier)
  - `dimension_scores` (per-dimension health objects)
  - `subjective_integrity` (anti-gaming metadata)
  - `score_confidence` (coverage impact on reliability)
  - `potentials` (detector check counts)
  - `scan_history` (rolling 20-entry log of scans)
  - `findings` (all discovered issues with status/resolution)

**Atomic persistence:**
- Temp file write → `os.replace()` (atomic on POSIX)
- Previous state backed up to `.json.bak`
- Default path: `.desloppify/state.json`

**Scoring happens before write:**
- `save_state()` calls `_recompute_stats()` first
- Ensures scores are current when persisted
- Validates invariants before commit

---

### 5. **Query Output: `write_query()` and Build Payload**

**Location:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/core/query.py`

**Two-layer query API:**

#### Layer 1: Core write function
```python
def write_query(data: dict, *, query_file: Path) -> OutputResult:
    """Write structured query payloads with config context."""
    if "config" not in data:
        data["config"] = config_for_query(load_config())
    safe_write_text(query_file, json.dumps(data, indent=2, default=json_default) + "\n")
```
- Writes to `query.json` (default: `.desloppify/query.json`)
- Automatically injects config context
- Returns `OutputResult(ok, status, message)`

#### Layer 2: Command-level builder (`scan_artifacts.py`)
```python
def build_scan_query_payload(state, config, profile, diff, warnings, narrative, merge, noise):
    """Build canonical query payload after scan."""
    scores = score_snapshot(state)  # Load all 4 scores
    return {
        "command": "scan",
        "overall_score": scores.overall,
        "objective_score": scores.objective,
        "strict_score": scores.strict,
        "verified_strict_score": scores.verified,
        "prev_overall_score": merge.prev_overall,  # Pre-scan baseline
        "prev_objective_score": merge.prev_objective,
        "prev_strict_score": merge.prev_strict,
        "prev_verified_strict_score": merge.prev_verified,
        "profile": profile,
        "diff": diff,
        "stats": state["stats"],
        "open_scope": open_scope_breakdown(...),
        "warnings": warnings,
        "dimension_scores": state.get("dimension_scores"),
        "score_breakdown": compute_health_breakdown(...),
        "subjective_integrity": state.get("subjective_integrity"),
        "score_confidence": state.get("score_confidence"),
        "potentials": state.get("potentials"),
        "scan_coverage": state.get("scan_coverage"),
        "zone_distribution": state.get("zone_distribution"),
        "narrative": narrative,
        "config": config_for_query(config),
    }
```

**Commands that generate query.json:**
- `scan` — main detection run with full payload
- `next` — best-effort action recommendation (scorecard dimensions + subjective measures)
- `status` — current health snapshot
- `plan` — planned improvements with dimension health table
- `review` — subjective assessment workflow
- Others that update findings/scores

**Payload file location:** `.desloppify/query.json`

### 6. **Scorecard Badge Generation**

**Location:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/output/scorecard.py`, line 43

**Signature:**
```python
def generate_scorecard(state: dict, output_path: str | Path) -> Path:
```

**What it does:**
1. Reads `overall_score` and `strict_score` from state (via `get_overall_score()`, `get_strict_score()`)
2. Loads active scorecard dimensions from `prepare_scorecard_dimensions(state)`
3. Renders a landscape PNG (780×variable height):
   - **Left panel:** Title, overall/strict scores, project name, version
   - **Right panel:** Dimension table (2 columns, limited rows)
   - **Theme:** Color-coded by score, custom font, grid layout
4. Saves to output path (default: `scorecard.png`)

**Triggers:**
- `emit_scorecard_badge()` in `scan_artifacts.py` (called by `cmd_scan`)
- Conditionally disabled by:
  - `--no-badge` flag
  - Config: `generate_scorecard: false`
  - Env: `DESLOPPIFY_NO_BADGE=1`
- Optional dependency: PIL (falls back gracefully)

**Output:** Persistent PNG image (example: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/assets/scorecard.png`)

### 8. **Anti-Gaming: Subjective Integrity Policy**

**Location:** `_apply_subjective_integrity_policy()` in `scoring.py`, lines 86–119

**What it does:**
- Detects when multiple subjective assessments cluster suspiciously on the target score
- Threshold: 2+ dimensions within ±5% of target → penalize all matched scores to 0
- Records penalty metadata: `matched_count`, `matched_dimensions`, `reset_dimensions`, `integrity_penalty: "target_match_reset"`

**Metadata in state:**
```python
state["subjective_integrity"] = {
    "status": "disabled" | "pass" | "warn" | "penalized",
    "target_score": float | None,
    "matched_count": int,
    "matched_dimensions": list[str],
    "reset_dimensions": list[str],
}
```

---

### 9. **Dimension Health Table & Score Breakdown**

**Location:** `compute_health_breakdown()` in `results/core.py`, lines 161–289

**Produces detailed transparency report:**
```python
{
    "overall_score": 87.5,
    "mechanical_fraction": 0.40,   # Weight of mechanical dims
    "subjective_fraction": 0.60,   # Weight of subjective dims
    "mechanical_avg": 91.2,        # Mechanical pool average
    "subjective_avg": 85.1,        # Subjective pool average
    "entries": [
        {
            "name": "File health",
            "pool": "mechanical",
            "score": 94.3,
            "checks": 1247,
            "sample_factor": 1.0,
            "configured_weight": 2.0,
            "effective_weight": 2.0,
            "pool_share": 0.25,
            "overall_per_point": 0.10,
            "overall_contribution": 9.43,
            "overall_drag": 0.57,
        },
        # ...
    ]
}
```
- **sample_factor:** Dampens dimensions with < 200 checks (confidence adjustment)
- **pool_share:** Dimension's weight relative to its pool (mech/subj)
- **overall_per_point:** How much 1 point in this dimension moves overall score
- **contribution/drag:** Actual impact on final score

---

### 10. **Dimension Weights & Scoring Constants**

**Location:** `policy/core.py`, lines 127–192

**Mechanical dimensions** (40% of overall):
- File health: 2.0× weight
- Code quality: 1.0× weight
- Duplication: 1.0× weight
- Test health: 1.0× weight
- Security: 1.0× weight

**Subjective dimensions** (60% of overall):
- High/mid elegance: 22.0× each (architectural north star)
- Contracts, type safety: 12.0× each
- Abstraction fit: 8.0×
- Logic clarity, design coherence: 6–10×
- Structure nav, error consistency, naming, AI debt: 1–5×

**Scoring modes:**
- **Lenient** (default): auto_resolved = fixed
- **Strict**: auto_resolved = open (requires manual confirmation)
- **Verified strict**: only fixes in latest scan count (prevents gaming)

**Sample dampening:**
- MIN_SAMPLE = 200 checks
- If checks < 200: weight *= checks / 200

## Summary Flow Diagram

```
Scan Results (detector findings)
    ↓
merge_scan()
    ├─ upsert_findings() → update state.findings
    ├─ auto_resolve_disappeared() → mark old issues resolved
    ├─ mark_stale_on_mechanical_change() → invalidate subjective assessments
    ├─ _recompute_stats()  ← MAIN SCORING ORCHESTRATOR
    │   ├─ _count_findings() → state.stats (counters, by_tier)
    │   └─ _update_objective_health()
    │       ├─ compute_score_bundle() → ScoreBundle
    │       │   ├─ compute_dimension_scores_by_mode()
    │       │   │   ├─ detector_stats_by_mode() per detector
    │       │   │   ├─ append_subjective_dimensions()
    │       │   │   └─ → lenient/strict/verified_strict scores
    │       │   ├─ compute_health_score(lenient) → overall_score
    │       │   ├─ compute_health_score(mechanical) → objective_score
    │       │   ├─ compute_health_score(strict) → strict_score
    │       │   └─ compute_health_score(verified_strict) → verified_strict_score
    │       ├─ _apply_subjective_integrity_policy() → anti-gaming
    │       └─ _apply_scan_coverage_to_dimension_scores() → score_confidence
    │           state.dimension_scores, state.overall_score, state.strict_score, ...
    └─ _append_scan_history() → rolling 20-entry log
    ↓
save_state()
    ├─ _recompute_stats() [again, ensures freshness]
    ├─ validate_state_invariants()
    └─ atomic write to .desloppify/state.json + .json.bak
    ↓
build_scan_query_payload()
    ├─ score_snapshot(state) → 4 canonical scores
    ├─ compute_health_breakdown() → detailed pool analysis
    └─ write_query(.desloppify/query.json)
    ↓
emit_scorecard_badge()
    ├─ generate_scorecard(state, 'scorecard.png')
    │   ├─ get_overall_score() + get_strict_score()
    │   ├─ prepare_scorecard_dimensions()
    │   ├─ PIL.ImageDraw → landscape PNG
    │   └─ write to scorecard.png
```

---

| Path | Purpose |
|------|---------|
| `desloppify/engine/_state/scoring.py` | `_recompute_stats()`, health computation, integrity policy |
| `desloppify/engine/_state/persistence.py` | `save_state()`, atomic I/O, backup logic |

This architecture ensures scoring is **deterministic, auditable, and resilient to gaming** through anti-gaming penalties, strict verification modes, and sample dampening for low-confidence dimensions.

## State Mutation and Output Patterns Report

Based on my exploration of the desloppify codebase, here are the key patterns for state mutation, output, and command architecture:

### 1. CommandRuntime Pattern
**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/helpers/runtime.py`

```python
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class CommandRuntime:
    """Explicit runtime dependencies shared by command handlers."""
    config: dict[str, Any]
    state: dict[str, Any]
    state_path: Path | None
```

**Usage Pattern:**
- Commands call `command_runtime(args)` to get a `CommandRuntime` instance
- This loads config and state in a single operation
- Example: `runtime = command_runtime(args); state = runtime.state; config = runtime.config`
- Used by: `show_cmd.py`, `status_cmd.py`, `config_cmd.py`, `resolve_cmd.py`, `review/*`

### 2. State Loading & Saving Pattern
**Files:** 
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_state/persistence.py` (save_state, load_state)
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/helpers/state.py` (state_path helpers)

**Key Functions:**
- `state_path(args)` → resolves state file path from args or auto-detects language
- `load_state(path)` → loads JSON, handles corruption with `.json.bak` fallback, returns empty state on error
- `save_state(state, path)` → atomic write (temp + os.replace), creates `.json.bak` backup, recomputes stats

**Pattern in Commands:**
```python
# Pattern 1: Direct load/save
state_file = state_path(args)
state = state_mod.load_state(state_file)
# ... mutate state ...
state_mod.save_state(state, state_file)

# Pattern 2: Using CommandRuntime
runtime = command_runtime(args)
state = runtime.state
state_path_file = runtime.state_path
# ... mutate state ...
state_mod.save_state(state, state_path_file)
```

**Commands that save state:**
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/fix/apply_flow.py` (lines 81, 102)
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/resolve/cmd.py` (multiple saves)
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/import_cmd.py`
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/scan/scan_workflow.py`

### 3. Query Output Pattern
**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/helpers/query.py`

```python
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class QueryWriter:
    query_file: Path
    def write(self, data: dict) -> OutputResult:
        return _core_write_query(data, query_file=self.query_file)
    def write_best_effort(self, data: dict, *, context: str) -> OutputResult:
        # emits warning on failure
```

**Functions:**
- `write_query(data)` → writes to default query.json with best-effort policy

### 9. Dimension-Based Scoring
**File:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/scoring.py`

Exports:
